Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

_source line 5207 · 2026-05-20T04:50:21.805Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
This is a critical finding. `run_5method_analysis.py` on westd-19828 still uses the OLD pair config:

```python
DOWNSTREAMS = ["distinct_1", "distinct_2", "distinct_3", "perplexity"]
```

This is the **pre-D102** config. D102 requires:
```
DOWNSTREAMS = ["distinct_1", "distinct_2", "distinct_3", "mauve"]
```

So if D120 dose-response recalculation uses `run_5method_analysis.py` as-is on westd-19828, it will use the WRONG pair config (perplexity instead of mauve). 

This directly violates D102's requirements. Now I need to check what westd-17855's batch_analysis.py is configured to use.
````

**westd-19828 run_5method_analysis.py**: `DOWNSTREAMS = ["distinct_1", "distinct_2", "distinct_3", "perplexity"]` — **pre-D102, NOT compliant** ✗

The D120 ROADMAP says to use `run_5method.py` for the unified pipeline recalculation. But:


- `run_5method_analysis.py` only exists on westd-19828 and uses perplexity instead of mauve, though it does have the correct 3σ threshold and direction post-M035 fix
- `batch_analysis.py` on westd-17855 has the D102 pairs with mauve but uses an inflated threshold that behaves like 2σ with a 4/8 false positive rate at null

Neither script fully satisfies all the compliance requirements—run_5method.py gets the threshold and direction right but uses the wrong downstream pair, while batch_analysis.py has the right pair but the wrong threshold.

The D120 recalculation can't proceed with either script as-is; I need to patch one of them first—either swap perplexity for mauve in run_5method.py or fix batch_analysis.py's threshold to true 3σ. This is critical because proceeding with run_5method.py without fixing the pair config means the unified recalculation will still be incorrect. I should flag this to the Director immediately.

Also noticing that westd-19828 is still idle even though the ROADMAP shows it as "启动中" with an update timestamp of 04:49 UTC, but both GPUs remain inactive.

```text
This is a thorough investigation of why TY and diff_xcorr values differ across the two analysis scripts. Let me analyze the findings:

**Key findings:**

1. **RC-1: DOWNSTREAMS mismatch** — Confirms what I already flagged to Director: run_5method uses perplexity, batch_analysis uses mauve. 2 of 8 pairs are computed on different data.

2. **RC-2: Direction check sign reversal** — run_5method_s43.py STILL uses `peak_lag > 0` (wrong, violates C009/M035), while batch_analysis.py uses `peak_lag < 0` (correct). 

So run_5method_s43.py is a variant that wasn't fixed with M035, which is critical — but this raises a question about the M037 audit. If that audit was supposedly done with run_5method_analysis.py on westd-19828, and this script still has the wrong direction check, then either the audit used a different version or the _s43 suffix indicates this is a separate file altogether that I need to investigate further.

Looking at what the worker found, there are actually multiple scripts in play here: run_5method_s43.py versus batch_analysis.py, and these are distinct from run_5method_analysis.py that I was examining earlier. The _s43 version appears to be a hardcoded variant created for specific experiments that never received the M035 fixes. Beyond the direction check issue, there are four fundamental implementation differences in how TY Granger is calculated between the scripts, making those values essentially incomparable. PermG remains the most reliable metric since it uses identical code paths across both scripts with no dependence on the ADF or direction filter. The worker's recommendations are to deprecate run_5method_s43.py, fix the _determine_d_max bug in batch_analysis.py, and acknowledge that TY becomes unreliable for voting scenarios with K≥3.

Now I'm concerned about whether the M037 audit used the wrong script with the incorrect direction logic. The _determine_d_max bug in batch_analysis.py is also problematic—it silently catches exceptions and defaults to d_max=1 as a fallback, which isn't actually testing stationarity. This means batch_analysis.py's TY values are unreliable too. If we drop TY from K≥3 voting as recommended, we're shifting from "≥3 of 5 methods" to "≥3 of 4 methods," which changes the voting threshold significantly.

For D120, there's confusion about which script version to use for recalculation—run_5method_analysis.py has correct direction but wrong pairs, while run_5method_s43.py has both issues. I need to check the investigation report and focus on three things: whether M037 was compromised, how this affects the D120 plan, and whether TY is reliable enough to keep.
```

````text
1	# TY/diff_xcorr 跨脚本不一致 Root Cause 调查报告
2	
3	**日期**: 2026-05-20
4	**状态**: P0 CRITICAL — 已定位 root cause
5	**调查范围**: `run_5method_s43.py` vs `batch_analysis.py`
6	**验证数据**: `alpha_sweep/a060_s43_fp32` (T=10), `alpha_sweep/a050_s43_fp32` (T=11)
7	
8	---
9	
10	## 1. 关键参数/方法差异表
11	
12	| 维度 | run_5method_s43.py | batch_analysis.py | 影响 |
13	|------|-------------------|-------------------|------|
14	| **DOWNSTREAMS** | distinct_1/2/3, **perplexity** | distinct_1/2/3, **mauve** | 8 pair 中 2 pair 完全不同 |
15	| **LOWER_IS_WORSE** | 不含 mauve | 含 mauve | orient 方向不同（但 diff_xcorr 对称，不影响结果） |
16	| **Direction check** | `peak_lag > 0` (**WRONG**, 违反 C009) | `peak_lag < 0` (正确) | naive/diff 计数完全反转 |
17	| **TY ADF regression** | 默认 `"c"` (constant only) | `"ct"` (constant + trend) | d_max 判定不同 |
18	| **TY ADF maxlag** | `min(2, T//3-1)` | `min(3, T//3-1)` | batch_analysis 在短序列上触发 bug |
19	| **TY d_max 逻辑** | 简单 p>0.05→1, 封顶 1 | 迭代差分 _determine_d_max, 封顶 2 | **batch_analysis 有 bug**（见下） |
20	| **TY lag 选择** | AIC 搜索 p∈{1,2} | 固定 max_lag=1 | 模型结构不同 |
21	| **TY 检验统计量** | F-test: `F = ((RSS_r-RSS_u)/q) / (RSS_u/df)` | Wald χ²: `W = n*(RSS_r-RSS_u)/RSS_u` | 小样本下 F 更准确，χ² 是渐近的 |
22	| **TY p-value 分布** | `stats.f.cdf(F, q, df_resid)` | `stats.chi2.cdf(W, q)` | 自由度处理完全不同 |
23	| **PermG 实现** | 相同 | 相同 | **一致** |
24	| **FDR 校正范围** | diff_xcorr, perm_granger | diff_xcorr, perm_granger_fwd, perm_granger_rev | 实质等价 |

26	---
27	
28	## 2. Per-Pair 结果对比
29	
30	### 2.1 TY Granger (alpha_sweep/a060_s43_fp32, T=10)

62	### 2.3 diff_xcorr (两实验均确认)
63	
64	**Per-pair 结果完全一致** — 两套脚本的 diff_xcorr 核心计算（差分、pearsonr、peak_lag 选择）相同。计数差异 **100% 来自 direction check**：
65	
66	| 实验 | peak_lag > 0 (r5, WRONG) | peak_lag < 0 (ba, CORRECT) |
67	|------|--------------------------|---------------------------|
68	| a060_s43_fp32 | 0/8 | 6/8 |
69	| a050_s43_fp32 | 0/8 | 6/8 |
70	
71	run_5method 在这两个实验上 diff_xcorr 会报 **0/8**（因 forward 信号全在 lag<0），而 batch_analysis 报 **6/8**。
72	
73	---
74	
75	## 3. Root Cause 汇总
76	
77	### RC-1 (CRITICAL): DOWNSTREAMS 列表不同
78	
79	- `run_5method_s43.py`: distinct_1/2/3 + **perplexity**
80	- `batch_analysis.py`: distinct_1/2/3 + **mauve**
81	- 8 pair 中 2 pair（canary→perplexity vs canary→mauve）在不同数据上计算
82	- 无法直接对比
83	
84	### RC-2 (CRITICAL): Direction check 符号反转 — 违反 C009/M035
85	
86	- `run_5method_s43.py` 共识计数: `(peak_lag or 0) > 0` → **反向因果**
87	- `batch_analysis.py` 共识计数: `(peak_lag or 0) < 0` → **正向因果**（正确）
88	- xcorr 约定: lag<0 = canary leads downstream = forward causation
89	- 影响: naive_xcorr 和 diff_xcorr 的 forward significant 计数完全反转
90	- 这是 D111/M035 sign reversal bug 的残留 — batch_analysis 已修正，run_5method 未修正
91	
92	### RC-3 (CRITICAL): TY Granger 实现完全不同
93	
94	**3a: ADF 回归类型**
95	- run_5method: `regression="c"` (constant) → ece 等单调序列容易判为平稳 (d_max=0)
96	- batch_analysis: `regression="ct"` (constant+trend) → 更保守，有 trend 的序列更难拒绝单位根
97	
98	**3b: _determine_d_max BUG（batch_analysis）**
99	
100	```
101	_determine_d_max 流程 (T=10):
102	  d=0: ADF(T=10, maxlag=2, ct) → 成功, p>0.05 → 差分
103	  d=1: ADF(T=9, maxlag=2, ct) → CRASH!
104	       约束: maxlag < nobs/2 - 1 - ntrend = 9/2 - 1 - 2 = 1.5
105	       maxlag=2 > 1.5 → ValueError
106	  except → return d=1  ← 不是真正的平稳性判定!
107	```
108	
109	大多数 metric 获得 d_max=1 是因为 **ADF 异常被静默吞掉**，不是因为真正需要一阶差分。
110	
111	**3c: Lag 阶数选择**
112	- run_5method: AIC 选择 p∈{1,2}，可能选 p=2 → 更多参数、更高拟合
113	- batch_analysis: 固定 max_lag=1 → 更简约
114	
115	**3d: 检验统计量**
116	- run_5method: F-test（有限样本精确分布）
117	- batch_analysis: Wald χ²（渐近分布）
118	- T=10-11 远未达到 χ² 渐近条件，两者 p-value 可差 1-2 个数量级
119	
120	### RC-4: 为什么 PermG 一致
121	
122	1. **代码路径相同**: 两套脚本的 `perm_granger()` / `_granger_f_stat()` / `_build_lag_matrix()` 实现完全一致
123	2. **参数相同**: max_lag=1, n_perm=5000, seed=42, difference=True
124	3. **无 direction filter**: PermG 天然有方向性（测试 x→y），不需要 peak_lag 方向判定
125	4. **不依赖 ADF/d_max**: 内部自行差分，避开了 ADF 不一致问题
126	5. **FDR 校正一致**: 两套都对 PermG p-value 做 BH FDR
127	
128	---
129	
130	## 4. 推荐
131	
132	### 4.1 选择哪套脚本
133	
134	**batch_analysis.py 更接近正确**，但仍有 bug 需修：
135	
136	| 项目 | run_5method_s43.py | batch_analysis.py |
137	|------|-------------------|-------------------|
138	| Direction check | ✗ (peak_lag>0, 违反 C009) | ✓ (peak_lag<0) |
139	| DOWNSTREAMS | ✗ (用 perplexity，不含 mauve) | ✓ (用 mauve) |
140	| TY d_max | 简单但无 bug | **有 _determine_d_max 异常 bug** |
141	| TY test stat | F-test（小样本更优） | χ²（渐近，T≤11 不适用） |
142	| 通用性 | 硬编码路径/实验 | 参数化 CLI |
143	
144	### 4.2 需要修复的问题
145	
146	1. **[P0] 废弃 run_5method_s43.py** — 方向错误、downstreams 错误、硬编码路径。不应再用于任何分析
147	2. **[P0] 修复 batch_analysis.py 的 _determine_d_max bug**:
148	   ```python
149	   # 修正: 考虑 regression="ct" 的 ntrend=2 约束
150	   def _determine_d_max(series, max_d=2, alpha=0.05):
151	       for d in range(max_d + 1):
152	           try:
153	               nobs = len(series)
154	               ntrend = 2  # "ct" = constant + trend
155	               safe_maxlag = max(1, nobs // 2 - 1 - ntrend - 1)
156	               result = adfuller(series, maxlag=safe_maxlag,
157	                                 regression="ct", autolag="AIC")
158	               if result[1] < alpha:
159	                   return d
160	               series = np.diff(series)
161	           except Exception:
162	               return min(d, 1)  # fallback 但不超过 1
163	       return max_d
164	   ```
165	3. **[P1] TY 检验统计量**: 对 T≤15 的短序列，改用 F-test 代替 χ²，或直接标注 TY 为 "underpowered"
166	4. **[P2] 考虑 TY 降级**: 鉴于 C003 已确认 TY 在低 α 下种子不稳定，且 _determine_d_max 有系统性 bug，建议 K≥3 投票中降低 TY 权重或排除
167	
168	### 4.3 对 K≥3 投票的影响评估
169	
170	| 方法 | 可信度 | 问题 |
171	|------|--------|------|
172	| **PermG** | ✓ 高 | 两套脚本一致，无 direction/ADF 依赖 |
173	| **threshold_onset** | ✓ 中高 | 两套实现一致（D105 3σ 标准另行校验） |
174	| **diff_xcorr** | ⚠ 依赖脚本 | 核心计算一致，但 run_5method 方向错误导致计数反转 |
175	| **naive_xcorr** | ⚠ 依赖脚本 | 同上 |
176	| **TY Granger** | ✗ 不可信 | 两套实现给出相反结论，batch_analysis 有 d_max bug，run_5method 有方向 bug |
177	
178	**结论**: 使用 run_5method 产出的任何包含 TY 或 diff/naive direction check 的 K≥3 数值**全部不可信**。batch_analysis 的 diff_xcorr 和 PermG 可信，但 TY 需修 bug 后重跑。
179	
180	**K≥3 修正策略**:
181	- 短期: K≥3 仅基于 PermG + threshold_onset + diff_xcorr (batch_analysis 版)，3 method 中 K≥2 为显著
182	- 中期: 修复 _determine_d_max 后重跑 TY，恢复 5-method 投票

      "content": "# TY/diff_xcorr 跨脚本不一致 Root Cause 调查报告\n\n**日期**: 2026-05-20\n**状态**: P0 CRITICAL — 已定位 root cause\n**调查范围**: `run_5method_s43.py` vs `batch_analysis.py`\n**验证数据**: `alpha_sweep/a060_s43_fp32` (T=10), `alpha_sweep/a050_s43_fp32` (T=11)\n\n---\n\n## 1. 关键参数/方法差异表\n\n| 维度 | run_5method_s43.py | batch_analysis.py | 影响 |\n|------|-------------------|-------------------|------|\n| **DOWNSTREAMS** | distinct_1/2/3, **perplexity** | distinct_1/2/3, **mauve** | 8 pair 中 2 pair 完全不同 |\n| **LOWER_IS_WORSE** | 不含 mauve | 含 mauve | orient 方向不同（但 diff_xcorr 对称，不影响结果） |\n| **Direction check** | `peak_lag > 0` (**WRONG**, 违反 C009) | `peak_lag < 0` (正确) | naive/diff 计数完全反转 |\n| **TY ADF regression** | 默认 `\"c\"` (constant only) | `\"ct\"` (constant + trend) | d_max 判定不同 |\n| **TY ADF maxlag** | `min(2, T//3-1)` | `min(3, T//3-1)` | batch_analysis 在短序列上触发 bug |\n| **TY d_max 逻辑** | 简单 p>0.05→1, 封顶 1 | 迭代差分 _determine_d_max, 封顶 2 | **batch_analysis 有 bug**（见下） |\n| **TY lag 选择** | AIC 搜索 p∈{1,2} | 固定 max_lag=1 | 模型结构不同 |\n| **TY 检验统计量** | F-test: `F = ((RSS_r-RSS_u)/q) / (RSS_u/df)` | Wald χ²: `W = n*(RSS_r-RSS_u)/RSS_u` | 小样本下 F 更准确，χ² 是渐近的 |\n| **TY p-value 分布** | `stats.f.cdf(F, q, df_resid)` | `stats.chi2.cdf(W, q)` | 自由度处理完全不同 |\n| **PermG 实现** | 相同 | 相同 | **一致** |\n| **FDR 校正范围** | diff_xcorr, perm_granger | diff_xcorr, perm_granger_fwd, perm_granger_rev | 实质等价 |\n\n---\n\n## 2. Per-Pair 结果对比\n\n### 2.1 TY Granger (alpha_sweep/a060_s43_fp32, T=10)\n\n| Pair | r5 sig | r5 p | r5 d_max | ba sig | ba p | ba d_max | 一致? |\n|------|--------|------|----------|--------|------|----------|-------|\n| token_entropy→distinct_1 | False | N/A* | 1 | False | 0.607 | 1 | == |\n| token_entropy→distinct_2 | False | N/A* | 1 | **True** | 0.014 | 1 | **!!** |\n| token_entropy→distinct_3 | False | 0.272 | 1 | **True** | 0.028 | 1 | **!!** |\n| token_entropy→mauve | False | 0.966 | 1 | False | 0.940 | 1 | == |\n| ece→distinct_1 | False | 0.261 | **0** | **True** | 0.003 | **1** | **!!** |\n| ece→distinct_2 | **True** | **2e-6** | **0** | False | 0.060 | **1** | **!!** |\n| ece→distinct_3 | **True** | **4e-6** | **0** | False | 0.579 | **1** | **!!** |\n| ece→mauve | False | 0.154 | 1 | **True** | 0.002 | 1 | **!!** |\n\n\\*N/A = n too small (p_aug 过大导致残差自由度不足)\n\n**TY 计数**: run_5method = **2/8**, batch_analysis = **4/8** (6/8 pair 结果不同)\n\n### 2.2 TY Granger (alpha_sweep/a050_s43_fp32, T=11)\n\n| Pair | r5 sig | r5 p | r5 d_max | ba sig | ba p | ba d_max | 一致? |\n|------|--------|------|----------|--------|------|----------|-------|\n| token_entropy→distinct_1 | True | 0.045 | 1 | True | 0.000 | 0 | == |\n| token_entropy→distinct_2 | False | 0.449 | 1 | **True** | **4e-6** | 0 | **!!** |\n| token_entropy→distinct_3 | False | 0.993 | 1 | False | 0.266 | 2 | == |\n| token_entropy→mauve | False | 0.791 | 1 | False | 0.661 | 2 | == |\n| ece→distinct_1 | False | 0.052 | 1 | **True** | **4e-5** | 1 | **!!** |\n| ece→distinct_2 | False | 0.349 | 1 | **True** | 0.006 | 1 | **!!** |\n| ece→distinct_3 | False | 0.335 | 1 | **True** | **0.000** | 2 | **!!** |\n| ece→mauve | False | 0.334 | 1 | False | 0.225 | 2 | == |\n\n**TY 计数**: run_5method = **1/8**, batch_analysis = **5/8**\n\n### 2.3 diff_xcorr (两实验均确认)\n\n**Per-pair 结果完全一致** — 两套脚本的 diff_xcorr 核心计算（差分、pearsonr、peak_lag 选择）相同。计数差异 **100% 来自 direction check**：\n\n| 实验 | peak_lag > 0 (r5, WRONG) | peak_lag < 0 (ba, CORRECT) |\n|------|--------------------------|---------------------------|\n| a060_s43_fp32 | 0/8 | 6/8 |\n| a050_s43_fp32 | 0/8 | 6/8 |\n\nrun_5method 在这两个实验上 diff_xcorr 会报 **0/8**（因 forward 信号全在 lag<0），而 batch_analysis 报 **6/8**。\n\n---\n\n## 3. Root Cause 汇总\n\n### RC-1 (CRITICAL): DOWNSTREAMS 列表不同\n\n- `run_5method_s43.py`: distinct_1/2/3 + **perplexity**\n- `batch_analysis.py`: distinct_1/2/3 + **mauve**\n- 8 pair 中 2 pair（canary→perplexity vs canary→mauve）在不同数据上计算\n- 无法直接对比\n\n### RC-2 (CRITICAL): Direction check 符号反转 — 违反 C009/M035\n\n- `run_5method_s43.py` 共识计数: `(peak_lag or 0) > 0` → **反向因果**\n- `batch_analysis.py` 共识计数: `(peak_lag or 0) < 0` → **正向因果**（正确）\n- xcorr 约定: lag<0 = canary leads downstream = forward causation\n- 影响: naive_xcorr 和 diff_xcorr 的 forward significant 计数完全反转\n- 这是 D111/M035 sign reversal bug 的残留 — batch_analysis 已修正，run_5method 未修正\n\n### RC-3 (CRITICAL): TY Granger 实现完全不同\n\n**3a: ADF 回归类型**\n- run_5method: `regression=\"c\"` (constant) → ece 等单调序列容易判为平稳 (d_max=0)\n- batch_analysis: `regression=\"ct\"` (constant+trend) → 更保守，有 trend 的序列更难拒绝单位根\n\n**3b: _determine_d_max BUG（batch_analysis）**\n\n```\n_determine_d_max 流程 (T=10):\n  d=0: ADF(T=10, maxlag=2, ct) → 成功, p>0.05 → 差分\n  d=1: ADF(T=9, maxlag=2, ct) → CRASH!\n       约束: maxlag < nobs/2 - 1 - ntrend = 9/2 - 1 - 2 = 1.5\n       maxlag=2 > 1.5 → ValueError\n  except → return d=1  ← 不是真正的平稳性判定!\n```\n\n大多数 metric 获得 d_max=1 是因为 **ADF 异常被静默吞掉**，不是因为真正需要一阶差分。\n\n**3c: Lag 阶数选择**\n- run_5method: AIC 选择 p∈{1,2}，可能选 p=2 → 更多参数、更高拟合\n- batch_analysis: 固定 max_lag=1 → 更简约\n\n**3d: 检验统计量**\n- run_5method: F-test（有限样本精确分布）\n- batch_analysis: Wald χ²（渐近分布）\n- T=10-11 远未达到 χ² 渐近条件，两者 p-value 可差 1-2 个数量级\n\n### RC-4: 为什么 PermG 一致\n\n1. **代码路径相同**: 两套脚本的 `perm_granger()` / `_granger_f_stat()` / `_build_lag_matrix()` 实现完全一致\n2. **参数相同**: max_lag=1, n_perm=5000, seed=42, difference=True\n3. **无 direction filter**: PermG 天然有方向性（测试 x→y），不需要 peak_lag 方向判定\n4. **不依赖 ADF/d_max**: 内部自行差分，避开了 ADF 不一致问题\n5. **FDR 校正一致**: 两套都对 PermG p-value 做 BH FDR\n\n---\n\n## 4. 推荐\n\n### 4.1 选择哪套脚本\n\n**batch_analysis.py 更接近正确**，但仍有 bug 需修：\n\n| 项目 | run_5method_s43.py | batch_analysis.py |\n|------|-------------------|-------------------|\n| Direction check | ✗ (peak_lag>0, 违反 C009) | ✓ (peak_lag<0) |\n| DOWNSTREAMS | ✗ (用 perplexity，不含 mauve) | ✓ (用 mauve) |\n| TY d_max | 简单但无 bug | **有 _determine_d_max 异常 bug** |\n| TY test stat | F-test（小样本更优） | χ²（渐近，T≤11 不适用） |\n| 通用性 | 硬编码路径/实验 | 参数化 CLI |\n\n### 4.2 需要修复的问题\n\n1. **[P0] 废弃 run_5method_s43.py** — 方向错误、downstreams 错误、硬编码路径。不应再用于任何分析\n2. **[P0] 修复 batch_analysis.py 的 _determine_d_max bug**:\n   ```python\n   # 修正: 考虑 regression=\"ct\" 的 ntrend=2 约束\n   def _determine_d_max(series, max_d=2, alpha=0.05):\n       for d in range(max_d + 1):\n           try:\n               nobs = len(series)\n               ntrend = 2  # \"ct\" = constant + trend\n               safe_maxlag = max(1, nobs // 2 - 1 - ntrend - 1)\n               result = adfuller(series, maxlag=safe_maxlag,\n                                 regression=\"ct\", autolag=\"AIC\")\n               if result[1] < alpha:\n                   return d\n               series = np.diff(series)\n           except Exception:\n               return min(d, 1)  # fallback 但不超过 1\n       return max_d\n   ```\n3. **[P1] TY 检验统计量**: 对 T≤15 的短序列，改用 F-test 代替 χ²，或直接标注 TY 为 \"underpowered\"\n4. **[P2] 考虑 TY 降级**: 鉴于 C003 已确认 TY 在低 α 下种子不稳定，且 _determine_d_max 有系统性 bug，建议 K≥3 投票中降低 TY 权重或排除\n\n### 4.3 对 K≥3 投票的影响评估\n\n| 方法 | 可信度 | 问题 |\n|------|--------|------|\n| **PermG** | ✓ 高 | 两套脚本一致，无 direction/ADF 依赖 |\n| **threshold_onset** | ✓ 中高 | 两套实现一致（D105 3σ 标准另行校验） |\n| **diff_xcorr** | ⚠ 依赖脚本 | 核心计算一致，但 run_5method 方向错误导致计数反转 |\n| **naive_xcorr** | ⚠ 依赖脚本 | 同上 |\n| **TY Granger** | ✗ 不可信 | 两套实现给出相反结论，batch_analysis 有 d_max bug，run_5method 有方向 bug |\n\n**结论**: 使用 run_5method 产出的任何包含 TY 或 diff/naive direction check 的 K≥3 数值**全部不可信**。batch_analysis 的 diff_xcorr 和 PermG 可信，但 TY 需修 bug 后重跑。\n\n**K≥3 修正策略**:\n- 短期: K≥3 仅基于 PermG + threshold_onset + diff_xcorr (batch_analysis 版)，3 method 中 K≥2 为显著\n- 中期: 修复 _determine_d_max 后重跑 TY，恢复 5-method 投票\n\n---\n\n## 5. 附录: 诊断脚本\n\n诊断脚本位于服务器: `/root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/diagnostic_compare.py`\n\n运行方式:\n```bash\npython diagnostic_compare.py results/alpha_sweep/a060_s43_fp32\n```\n",

from all_metrics.json if available, else aggregate gen_X/metrics.json.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    all_path = os.path.join(data_dir, \\\"all_metrics.json\\\")\\n    if os.path.exists(all_path):\\n        with open(all_path) as f:\\n            data = json.load(f)\\n    else:\\n        # Aggregate from per-generation metrics\\n        gen_dirs = sorted(glob_module.glob(os.path.join(data_dir, \\\"gen_*\\\")),\\n                         key=lambda x: int(os.path.basename(x).split(\\\"_\\\")[1]))\\n        data = []\\n        for gd in gen_dirs:\\n            mp = os.path.join(gd, \\\"metrics.json\\\")\\n            if os.path.exists(mp):\\n                with open(mp) as f:\\n                    data.append(json.load(f))\\n        if not data:\\n            raise FileNotFoundError(f\\\"No metrics found in {data_dir}\\\")\\n    data.sort(key=lambda x: x[\\\"generation\\\"])\\n    out = {}\\n    for k in CANARIES + DOWNSTREAMS:\\n        out[k] = np.array([d[k] for d in data])\\n    return out, len(data)\\n\\n\\ndef orient(series, name):\\n    return -series.copy() if name in LOWER_IS_WORSE else series.copy()\\n\\n\\ndef bh_fdr(p_values, alpha=0.05):\\n    pv = np.array(p_values, dtype=float)\\n    n = len(pv)\\n    valid = ~np.isnan(pv)\\n    adjusted = np.full(n, np.nan)\\n    if valid.sum() == 0:\\n        return adjusted.tolist(), [False] * n\\n    idx = np.where(valid)[0]\\n    p_valid = pv[idx]\\n    sorted_idx = np.argsort(p_valid)\\n    ranks = np.empty_like(sorted_idx)\\n    ranks[sorted_idx] = np.arange(1, len(p_valid) + 1)\\n    m = len(p_valid)\\n    adj = p_valid * m / ranks\\n    adj_sorted = adj[sorted_idx].copy()\\n    for i in range(m - 2, -1, -1):\\n        adj_sorted[i] = min(adj_sorted[i], adj_sorted[i + 1])\\n    adj_sorted = np.minimum(adj_sorted, 1.0)\\n    adj_final = np.empty(m)\\n    adj_final[sorted_idx] = adj_sorted\\n    for i, ix in enumerate(idx):\\n        adjusted[ix] = adj_final[i]\\n    significant = [(adjusted[i] < alpha if not np.isnan(adjusted[i]) else False) for i in range(n)]\\n    return adjusted.tolist(), significant\\n\\n\\n# Method 1: Naive cross-correlation\\ndef naive_xcorr(c, d, cn, dn, max_lag=5):\\n    x, y = orient(c, cn), orient(d, dn)\\n    n = len(x)\\n    best_lag, best_r, best_p = 0, 0.0, 1.0\\n    all_lags = []\\n    for lag in range(-max_lag, max_lag + 1):\\n        if lag >= 0:\\n            a, b = x[lag:], y[:n - lag]\\n        else:\\n            a, b = x[:n + lag], y[-lag:]\\n        if len(a) < 4:\\n            continue\\n        r, p = stats.pearsonr(a, b)\\n        all_lags.append({\\\"lag\\\": int(lag), \\\"r\\\": round(float(r), 4), \\\"p\\\": round(float(p), 6)})\\n        if abs(r) > abs(best_r):\\n            best_lag, best_r, best_p = lag, r, p\\n    return {\\n        \\\"method\\\": \\\"naive_xcorr\\\",\\n        \\\"peak_lag\\\": int(best_lag),\\n        \\\"r\\\": round(float(best_r), 4),\\n        \\\"p_value\\\": round(float(best_p), 6),\\n        \\\"significant\\\": bool(best_p < ALPHA),\\n        \\\"all_lags\\\": all_lags,\\n    }\\n\\n\\n# Method 2: Differenced cross-correlation\\ndef diff_xcorr(c, d, cn, dn, max_lag=5):\\n    dx, dy = np.diff(orient(c, cn)), np.diff(orient(d, dn))\\n    n = len(dx)\\n    best_lag, best_r, best_p = 0, 0.0, 1.0\\n    all_lags = []\\n    for lag in range(-max_lag, max_lag + 1):\\n        if lag >= 0:\\n            a, b = dx[lag:], dy[:n - lag]\\n        else:\\n            a, b = dx[:n + lag], dy[-lag:]\\n        if len(a) < 4:\\n            continue\\n        if np.std(a) < 1e-12 or np.std(b) < 1e-12:\\n            continue\\n        r, p = stats.pearsonr(a, b)\\n        all_lags.append({\\\"lag\\\": int(lag), \\\"r\\\": round(float(r), 4), \\\"p\\\": round(float(p), 6)})\\n        if abs(r) > abs(best_r):\\n            best_lag, best_r, best_p = lag, r, p\\n    return {\\n        \\\"method\\\": \\\"diff_xcorr\\\",\\n        \\\"peak_lag\\\": int(best_lag),\\n        \\\"r\\\": round(float(best_r), 4),\\n        \\\"p_value\\\": round(float(best_p), 6),\\n        \\\"significant\\\": bool(best_p < ALPHA),\\n        \\\"all_lags\\\": all_lags,\\n    }\\n\\n\\n# Method 3: Threshold onset (drift ratio >= 3x)\\ndef _onset_gen(series, name):\\n    s = orient(series, name)\\n    mu, sigma = s[0], np.std(s[:3]) if len(s) >= 3 else np.std(s)\\n    if sigma < 1e-12:\\n        sigma = np.std(s) if np.std(s) > 1e-12 else 1.0\\n    for i in range(1, len(s)):\\n        drift = abs(s[i] - def _onset_gen(series, name):

```

Continuing with the drift detection logic...

The function establishes a baseline from the first value and computes sigma from the first three points, then iterates through the series checking if any point drifts more than 3 standard deviations from that initial value—returning the index where this threshold is first exceeded. This is a 3σ drift detector, though it differs from the true D105 implementation since it anchors to the first observation rather than a rolling mean.

I'm realizing the distinction now: the current approach uses a fixed baseline from the start of the series, whereas D105 true 3σ should establish its threshold from the full baseline statistics under the null hypothesis, computing the mean and standard deviation across the entire series before checking for deviations. The implementation here is more of a simplified early-warning system that flags when values diverge significantly from the initial conditions.

Looking at the broader picture, switching to the proper D105 method would essentially eliminate threshold as a viable detection method since it would almost never trigger, making it a non-contributor to the K≥3 voting scheme. is 3/4 compliant. The key issue is that threshold_onset relies on 3x_sigma_drift instead of the true 3σ threshold from D105, which inflates its values. Since threshold essentially produces no hits under proper 3σ criteria (0/8 across experiments), the practical impact on K≥3 hinges on whether any experiments land exactly at the boundary with threshold as the sole supporting vote. For D120 recalculation using _d102.py, PermG and the cross-correlation methods would be correct, but threshold_onset and TY remain problematic—TY especially given the worker's findings about its unreliability across both scripts. I need to update the Director with the corrected findings about which script is actually more reliable for the D120 analysis.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Key findings from this worker:

1. **westd-19828:cuda:0**: PID 531127 running for 11h55m, in Generation 0 featurizing at ~59%. This is interesting — a process IS running on cuda:0, but it's been 12 hours and still on Gen 0? That sounds extremely slow or stuck.

Wait, actually the worker says "已运行 11h55m" and "处于 Generation 0 的 Featurizing 阶段 (~59%进度)". This doesn't match — featurizing should take minutes, not 12 hours. 

There's a timing discrepancy here. When I checked nvidia-smi on 19828 about 30 minutes ago, both GPUs showed 0% utilization and no memory usage. Now cuda:0 suddenly has an active process? Either this was just launched after my last check, or there's something off with the nvidia-smi readings from earlier. But the worker timestamp says the process has been running for 11 hours, which would put its start time around 12 hours ago — that doesn't align with what I observed.

Actually, this makes sense if the process is stuck in CPU-bound featurization work rather than GPU computation. Featurizing is typically a CPU operation that wouldn't show up in GPU memory or utilization, so the process could be alive and running while nvidia-smi shows zeros. That said, 12 hours spent in Generation 0 featurization is suspicious — that operation usually completes in minutes. The process might be stuck or running extremely slowly. Let me verify this is the plan_008 s43 experiment that was supposed to run on 19828. The featurizing timeline doesn't add up — that should take minutes, not hours. Generation 0 is just baseline evaluation with no training, so if it's still running after 12 hours, either the process is genuinely slow for this particular model and dataset, it's stuck, or the worker's log reading is inaccurate. I need to dig deeper into what's actually happening rather than trust the "normal operation" assessment.
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"5-method lead-lag analysis: generalized CLI version.\\n\\nUsage:\\n    python run_5method_analysis.py --data_dir results/alpha_sweep/a000_s43 --alpha 0.0 --seed 43\\n    python run_5method_analysis.py --data_dir results/alpha_sweep/a000_s43 --alpha 0.0 --seed 43 --dry-run\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nimport sys\\nimport glob as glob_module\\nimport numpy as np\\nfrom scipy import stats\\nfrom statsmodels.tsa.stattools import adfuller\\n\\nsys.path.insert(0, \\\"/root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse\\\")\\nfrom permutation_granger import permutation_granger_test\\n\\nN_PERM = 5000\\nALPHA = 0.05\\n\\nCANARIES = [\\\"token_entropy\\\", \\\"ece\\\"]\\nDOWNSTREAMS = [\\\"distinct_1\\\", \\\"distinct_2\\\", \\\"distinct_3\\\", \\\"mauve\\\"]\\n\\nHIGHER_IS_WORSE = {\\\"ece\\\"}\\nLOWER_IS_WORSE = {\\\"token_entropy\\\", \\\"distinct_1\\\", \\\"distinct_2\\\", \\\"distinct_3\\\", \\\"mauve\\\"}\\n\\n\\ndef load_series(data_dir):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Load metrics - from all_metrics.json if available, else aggregate gen_X/metrics.json.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    all_path = os.path.join(data_dir, \\\"all_metrics.json\\\")\\n    if os.path.exists(all_path):\\n        with open(all_path) as f:\\n            data = json.load(f)\\n    else:\\n        # Aggregate from per-generation metrics\\n        gen_dirs = sorted(glob_module.glob(os.path.join(data_dir, \\\"gen_*\\\")),\\n                         key=lambda x: int(os.path.basename(x).split(\\\"_\\\")[1]))\\n        data = []\\n        for gd in gen_dirs:\\n            mp = os.path.join(gd, \\\"metrics.json\\\")\\n            if os.path.exists(mp):\\n                with open(mp) as f:\\n                    data.append(json.load(f))\\n        if not data:\\n            raise FileNotFoundError(f\\\"No metrics found in {data_dir}\\\")\\n    data.sort(key=lambda x: x[\\\"generation\\\"])\\n    out = {}\\n    for k in CANARIES + DOWNSTREAMS:\\n        out[k] = np.array([d[k] for d in data])\\n    return out, len(data)\\n\\n\\ndef orient(series, name):\\n    return -series.copy() if name in LOWER_IS_WORSE else series.copy()\\n\\n\\ndef bh_fdr(p_values, alpha=0.05):\\n    pv = np.array(p_values, dtype=float)\\n    n = len(pv)\\n    valid = ~np.isnan(pv)\\n    adjusted = np.full(n, np.nan)\\n    if valid.sum() == 0:\\n        return adjusted.tolist(), [False] * n\\n    idx = np.where(valid)[0]\\n    p_valid = pv[idx]\\n    sorted_idx = np.argsort(p_valid)\\n    ranks = np.empty_like(sorted_idx)\\n    ranks[sorted_idx] = np.arange(1, len(p_valid) + 1)\\n    m = len(p_valid)\\n    adj = p_valid * m / ranks\\n    adj_sorted = adj[sorted_idx].copy()\\n    for i in range(m - 2, -1, -1):\\n        adj_sorted[i] = min(adj_sorted[i], adj_sorted[i + 1])\\n    adj_sorted = np.minimum(adj_sorted, 1.0)\\n    adj_final = np.empty(m)\\n    adj_final[sorted_idx] = adj_sorted\\n    for i, ix in enumerate(idx):\\n        adjusted[ix] = adj_final[i]\\n    significant = [(adjusted[i] < alpha if not np.isnan(adjusted[i]) else False) for i in range(n)]\\n    return adjusted.tolist(), significant\\n\\n\\n# Method 1: Naive cross-correlation\\ndef naive_xcorr(c, d, cn, dn, max_lag=5):\\n    x, y = orient(c, cn), orient(d, dn)\\n    n = len(x)\\n    best_lag, best_r, best_p = 0, 0.0, 1.0\\n    all_lags = []\\n    for lag in range(-max_lag, max_lag + 1):\\n        if lag >= 0:\\n            a, b = x[lag:], y[:n - lag]\\n        else:\\n            a, b = x[:n + lag], y[-lag:]\\n        if len(a) < 4:\\n            continue\\n        r, p = stats.pearsonr(a, b)\\n        all_lags.append({\\\"lag\\\": int(lag), \\\"r\\\": round(float(r), 4), \\\"p\\\": round(float(p), 6)})\\n        if abs(r) > abs(best_r):\\n            best_lag, best_r, best_p = lag, r, p\\n    return {\\n        \\\"method\\\": \\\"naive_xcorr\\\",\\n        \\\"peak_lag\\\": int(best_lag),\\n        \\\"r\\\": round(float(best_r), 4),\\n        \\\"p_value\\\": round(float(best_p), 6),\\n        \\\"significant\\\": bool(best_p < ALPHA),\\n        \\\"all_lags\\\": all_lags,\\n    }\\n\\n\\n# Method 2: Differenced cross-correlation\\ndef diff_xcorr(c, d, cn, dn, max_lag=5):\\n    dx, dy = np.diff(orient(c, cn)), np.diff(orient(d, dn))\\n    n = len(dx)\\n    best_lag, best_r, best_p = 0, 0.0, 1.0\\n    all_lags = []\\n    for lag in range(-max_lag, max_lag + 1):\\n        if lag >= 0:\\n            a, b = dx[lag:], dy[:n - lag]\\n        else:\\n            a, b = dx[:n + lag], dy[-lag:]\\n        if len(a) < 4:\\n            continue\\n        if np.std(a) < 1e-12 or np.std(b) < 1e-12:\\n            continue\\n        r, p = stats.pearsonr(a, b)\\n        all_lags.append({\\\"lag\\\": int(lag), \\\"r\\\": round(float(r), 4), \\\"p\\\": round(float(p), 6)})\\n        if abs(r) > abs(best_r):\\n            best_lag, best_r, best_p = lag, r, p\\n    return {\\n        \\\"method\\\": \\\"diff_xcorr\\\",\\n        \\\"peak_lag\\\": int(best_lag),\\n        \\\"r\\\": round(float(best_r), 4),\\n        \\\"p_value\\\": round(float(best_p), 6),\\n        \\\"significant\\\": bool(best_p < ALPHA),\\n        \\\"all_lags\\\": all_lags,\\n    }\\n\\n\\n# Method 3: Threshold onset (D105 true 3-sigma)\\ndef _onset_gen(series, name):\\n    s = orient(series, name)\\n    base, sigma = s[0], np.std(s)\\n    if sigma < 1e-12:\\n        return None\\n    thresh = base + 3.0 * sigma\\n    for i in range(1, len(s)):\\n        if s[i] > thresh:\\n            return i\\n    return None\\n\\n\\ndef threshold_onset(c, d, cn, dn):\\n    c_onset = _onset_gen(c, cn)\\n    d_onset = _onset_gen(d, dn)\\n    if c_onset is not None and d_onset is not None:\\n        lead_time = d_onset - c_onset\\n        significant = lead_time > 0\\n    else:\\n        lead_time = None\\n        significant = False\\n    return {\\n        \\\"method\\\": \\\"threshold_onset\\\",\\n        \\\"canary_onset\\\": c_onset,\\n        \\\"downstream_onset\\\": d_onset,\\n        \\\"lead_time\\\": lead_time,\\n        \\\"significant\\\": significant,\\n        \\\"threshold\\\": \\\"true_3sigma_d105\\\",\\n    }\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.43}}"}

I'm realizing there's a fundamental flaw in how D105 computes its threshold — by using the standard deviation of the entire series, including the contaminated generations, the threshold gets inflated when the collapse is severe, making it actually harder to detect when degradation begins. This is why the onset detection is failing; the baseline std should come from clean data before contamination takes effect, not from the polluted series itself.

The implementation does match the D105 spec as written, though, and the M037 audit confirms this conservative approach consistently produces 0/8 detections across experiments. I've verified the core pieces: the full-series std calculation, the one-directional check after orientation, the correct label assignment, and that both servers have the same implementation with matching line numbers.

Now I'm checking that the D102 mauve pairs on line 24 are still intact and confirming the direction check for peak_lag < 0 on lines 436/438 hasn't been disrupted. With those verified, the script should be fully compliant across D102, D105, D111, and FDR requirements, which means the D120 recalculation can move forward.

There are still a couple of loose ends—the TY Granger cross-script issue remains unresolved but it's a known limitation, and the old `run_5method_analysis.py` without the _d102 suffix still has incorrect pair configuration that should probably be deprecated to prevent confusion. Let me double-check that the 17855 version aligns with what I've just verified.
````

```python
def _onset_gen(series, name):
    s = orient(series, name)
    base, sigma = s[0], np.std(s)
    if sigma < 1e-12:
        return None
    thresh = base + 3.0 * sigma
    for i in range(1, len(s)):
        if s[i] > thresh:
            return i
```
